ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e10648	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen! Let's go back to the subject matter. What is it really about? Phase one has been very successful thanks to this house and many, many people in this country. We managed to reduce the enormous daily growth from 40, 45, 50, 55 percent to 0.3, 0.5, 0.7 percent. This has been a big undertaking, and that has been successful. ==References====External links== April the phase of the opening steps, and this phase has also been well achieved. There have been no significant increases in the spread and number of infections in these areas in which there has been reopening. Now we are in phase three, the stabilisation after the opening steps: This phase has always been marked by – and we assumed – that regional outbreaks can occur; we had this in Lower Austria, we had it in Vienna, we now have it in Upper Austria. And yes, this Upper Austria Cluster is not a small thing, it is special and we have to go extremely vigilant on it. The objective of all these regional outbreaks is to carry out a rapid demarcation of those persons who have received a positive test and have the corresponding disease signals. - That's the situation. We now have the goal in Austria that within 24 hours the survey of these persons must take place. I think it is very important to define this as a goal, because the longer it takes up to these surveys, the more it can be exclusions and, above all, the spread of the virus; that is, speed brings success. The longer we need in these surveys, in this contact person management, the more likely it is that there will be spreads, and we all want to avoid that. Now we have two options: firstly, the possibility that we have sufficient personnel for the people to question. This is obviously not the case in all regions, for otherwise we would not have received a request from Upper Austria asking for the executive to support it. The second option would be to train, train and train additional forces in this area so that they can support this contact person management. That's what we're doing. By September, there will be staff members of the military forces who will be able to provide rescue workers who will learn exactly this job and then, when it comes to a regional outbreak, be supportive. By September, however, we have the situation that we still have regional outbreaks, and we must get to grips with them as much as possible. On the situation – quite simply again, Mr. Loacker –: We have a long time ago as a legal situation what you are calling for in the amendment. We have the legal situation that the executive, at the request of a health authority, can carry out these surveys, the identity and the contacts concerning them and has done so in thousands of cases in Austria, and if the health authority does not want to do so, it does not order it. That is why the city of Vienna – and I accept and appreciate very much what Mr Hacker realizes as his own course – does not do that, and that is legitimate. [[Abg. Belakovich: Yes, he does well!]] If another country needs this in this situation, I think it is also legitimate and easy to implement. – These steps have already been implemented. Then the health official comes into the same room and asks one more question. - Does that make sense? Is this really efficient in an acute situation? It is precisely this: he can still ask this one question that he can still write down the answer to the question of symptoms, send the health authority and delete it in his area. I assume that this will be done and implemented correctly and legally in Austria. So please, as I am talking about the rule of law and major problems, I understand in principle, but in reality it really looks different from my point of view. I may need to make a statement, namely: We have, of course, agreed on a restrictive migration policy in the government programme. These two things – a restrictive migration policy and yet asylum procedures, which are sensitive and human rights-compliant and respect human dignity – are dealt with in this proposal, which does not contradict. We have highlighted two vulnerable groups here, because in practice we have seen that most of the problems are there. On the one hand, these are people with equal sexual orientation and, on the other, people who turn to Islam of another religion – and that is most often Christianity according to statistics. In many countries of the world – you may not believe it – these two groups are threatened with death and prison. If such a person comes to us, then in Austria he has an asylum ground under the Geneva Convention on Refugees, but he still encounters difficulties. I would now like to mention three problem areas in which such a person in Austria can have difficulties. The first is that he's being suspected of being able to stay here, of being suspected, or supposed to be giving sexual orientation only because he wants to have asylum. I agree that there will be cases in which such a thing is proposed. But we cannot allow a general suspicion to be pronounced. It is very important for the Authority to determine in a procedure what is right and what is not true, and in the course of this finding, it is first of all sensitive. This also concerns the question: It has come to our attention a number of difficulties with regard to questions which cannot simply be asked in such a way that they are not meaningful. The Federal Ministry of the Interior has set up its own unit for quality and training to address this issue. At the same time, however, we wished that the Authority's assessment would take seriously, for example, what a legally recognized church says. For example, if a legally recognized church says: I looked at it, I prepared it for a year, baptized it!, then of course it is relevant to the authority. And we want to underline this relevance. The same applies to civil society organisations that care for these people. They should also be included. – This is point one: no suspicion, no suspicion of proposed arguments. Point two: Refugees coming from these two affected groups keep reporting that their statements have been mistranslated that they have experienced that the translator, who comes out of the cultural circle of course, himself has reservations against, for example, the sexual orientation or the conversion of this person. It is very important that we ensure that the interpreters are suitable and do not bring personal aversions. A third problem is asylum homes. I have numbers from Germany that are not quite up-to-date – we do not have statistics – but they help to illustrate: In Germany, 750 attacks on conversions in asylum homes were reported in 2017. I know that 2017 is not 2020, it is Germany and not Austria, but you can take something from that figure. What kind of assaults are these? – For example, an exclusion from the use of the kitchen or the use of the shared bath, because it is said to be unclean. This is known from the countries of origin. That is what we have in the asylum homes. In 2017 the German Federal Criminal Office reported a total of 100 crimes against Christians in this context. That's what we need to look at. The Archdiocese of Vienna is the starting point for conversions of those who want to become Catholic, and there is an admission celebration once a year. Interestingly, she says that for security reasons she always wants to do this approval ceremony in different places, and never in St. Stephen's Cathedral, because it is too dangerous. The Evangelical Church speaks of similar events. All of this only throws lights on a bigger problem. What remains under the rule – and therefore our request: In the case of vulnerable groups, we must be particularly sensitive, we must be particularly vigilant and we must protect people as they need it. Fair asylum procedures, even if one is running a restrictive migration policy, are simply a question of the state of origin, human rights and human dignity. Thank you.

It is traded This is an important global concern. The Citizens' Initiative was sent to the Human Rights Committee for further treatment and discussed there. Nationally forced organ withdrawal and organ harvesting from prisoners as well as religious and ethnic minorities are currently sad and inconceivably brutal reality in the People's Republic of China. Even though the Chinese government claims the opposite, there are always strong indications that thousands of people are being killed to come to their organs – we are talking about the death penalty in the same breath. This shows that 1.5 million people were killed in China for the purpose of organ withdrawal over a period of about 15 years. ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e13486	Mrs Rosa Ecker has now come to speak. - Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e13507	Mr President. Mr. Minister! High house! Yes: no organs from China to Austria. We have now heard what the situation is like in China. What does it look like in Austria regarding transplants? – That’s a subject that we don’t like to deal with. In Austria, transplants are clearly regulated: the principles of donation, the transport of organs, traceability as well as the international organ exchange. In Austria, you are automatically organ donors in case of death. If you don't want to, you have to deposit it in writing, in the hospital file or in the register of objections. Organ and stem cell transplants make life easier for sick people and save lives. In Austria, this is done at four locations, namely at the University Clinics in Graz, Innsbruck and Vienna, as well as at the Oberösterreich hospital in the Elisabethinen hospital, where kidney transplants are made. There is a very good follow-up programme for live donors, who often come from the nearer family to ensure the health of organ donors. Living donors are also constantly being sought. According to the Austrian Ministry of Health, nearly 800 organs were transplanted in 2018. More than half of these were kidneys, followed by liver, lung, and heart donations. In Austria, there is really a clear legal regime. In order to keep the number of organ donors constant and possibly still increasing, there is a funding programme, which is also contained in the Hospital and Spa Act. This is necessary and important, because organs in Austria can only be donated voluntarily and free of charge, i.e. without by-products, without donations, without any other advantage – that is forbidden, just as all forms of organ trafficking are prohibited, the attempt is already punishable. Austria is one of the eight members of Eurotransplant; Organs are only transmitted via Eurotransplant, and there are very clear criteria – according to urgency, according to medical need. This means that organs used in Austria are safe and legally transplanted. In this way I would also like to share this with the spectators, who are perhaps somewhat concerned about the current discussion here. Like Mr Bayr, I am convinced that none of us would like to receive an organ that has become available through organ harvesting or illegal withdrawal in the case of this case. To calm down: There is also a request from the Ministry of Social Affairs from December 2018, in which it is noted that no organ from China has been transplanted in Austria until then. I assume that those who act in Austria continue to comply with the laws. The trade in organs must be strongly rejected. Austria supports numerous initiatives against trafficking in human beings for the purpose of organ withdrawal and against organ trafficking in itself. Of course, we agree with the concern of this citizens' initiative and with that request.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e13957	We're at 12. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs. Dr. Gudrun Kuglergelanget. - Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e13987	Mr President! Ladies and gentlemen! If we talk about trafficking in human beings – I really want to take a few things out now because I have to pay attention to the speaking time – then we are talking about one of the most lucrative business of organised crime, and Austria is double affected as a target country and as a transit country. We are talking about exploitation in various areas – exploitation in prostitution, bed and work – but also the issue of child trafficking is part of this area. I now take two examples out of all these topics. One is from the area of prostitution. We know, or we better say so, we appreciate that a third of the prostitutes who work in Austria do not voluntarily do so. We have a very special legal situation, because our asylum law provides that if someone makes an application for asylum, he or she can already report himself as a self-employed person after three months. Statistically, this is only happening in prostitution. This provision is a pull factor and assists retailers in bringing women from poorer countries mainly to Austria in order to bring them to this kind of traded prostitution. The police are controlling and trying to identify these people, these women, and help them. But there are national laws that make this impossible. I would like to point this out in the example of the Burgenland – and that is why I am now looking at it. towards SPÖ –: There is a new prostitution law there that determines that the police may only control if there is a legitimate suspicion, but it must not carry out routine controls. This is a very big problem and contradicts the European Parliament's directive on trafficking in human beings, in which it is said that the police should make such routine controls essential. This is no longer possible in Burgenland. I would like to give a very different example, namely from the trade in children. We know that in some European countries, mainly poorer countries, there is the possibility of glue maternity, and, accordingly, there is also an international lead maternity tourism. There are, of course, no rules for adoption where you can look very closely at whether this is the best solution for the child or not. Motherhood allows children to trade as a fake business by claiming that it is a bond-nutritional contract, while in reality there is no bond-nutrition at all. This makes it possible for children to trade. So that this doesn't remain so abstract, I'll give you an example: The Austrian authorities have recently been asked to provide assistance from the Czech authorities. They reported that a man who had been punished for child pornography several times, 60-year-old, brought a child in Prague by means of a maternity contract. It is assumed that it was just a fake business and that there was no real genetic relationship at all. This could not be controlled due to the legal situation. The Czech authorities now say that they do not know where this man is, nor where the child is, and ask if the Austrian authorities can help. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen, we must not allow such cases to happen. They read in the media what is happening in the field of child pornography in terrible things. We open doors and gates with such a legal situation for an abuse which you cannot and do not even want to imagine. Trafficking in human beings in all its forms is always an exploitation of incomes. It always means that there are people who are willing to do a lot, yes, everything to survive because they are poor, and it also means that there are people who have enough money to buy these things then. We can't watch that! Today's request is supported by all parties, as I have taken it from the committee. He says we have to do it. Austria, I've already said it, is transit country and destination country. We need to identify the victims, we need to help the victims, and we need a low level of access to advice, exit. There are several possibilities, that is all in our request. In conclusion, I would like to say one more thing: Austria has seen this problem for a long time, and we have created numerous mechanisms to work on this problem. I only call the Task Force, the national action plan we have, the central office in the Federal Criminal Office, the activities at European and international level, where Austria has management functions over and over again, and numerous victim protection facilities such as Lefö, Men Via, Ecpat and Solvodi. ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e14078	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! More than 2.4 million people are traded as commodities each year. They experience the worst forms of economic exploitation and a massive violation of their human rights. Trafficking in human beings is an enormous economic factor. The profits are estimated at 32 billion US dollars worldwide, and this is the case every year. Business with the goods of human beings is considered to be the third most dangerous criminal source of income, even after drug trafficking and arms trafficking. In the case of trafficking in human beings, the foreign exchange shall be: Whoever pays will be delivered to him – whether working or sex slaves, people for organ withdrawal, marriage or adoption. Age, gender and ethnic origin hardly play a role in this. The most common forms of exploitation, and this has previously been demonstrated by Mr Kugler in her case studies, are sexual exploitation and forced labour, often under slave-like conditions. 43 percent of the victims of trafficking in human beings are sold to the sex industry, i.e. forced prostitution; a third of the people are exploited in agriculture, in households or in other exploiting plants under humane conditions. Trafficking in human beings is a sum of human rights violations. Inalienable rights such as the right to life, freedom, equality, dignity, security, non-discrimination, health and all rights of employment protection are disregarded. Victims are severely traumatized by the psychological and physical torments and often drawn by these experiences for the rest of their lives, even though they have been able to escape the slave trade or exploitation situation. We therefore, of course, support this proposal and we are happy to work together. Extensive measures against trafficking in human beings also include our request for a social responsibility law which has been allocated to the Committee. With the help of this law, products manufactured by forced or child labour are no longer to be imported into Austria. Ladies and gentlemen, I also hope to have broad support for our proposal in the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs, because we agree: No human should be victims of trafficking in human beings!

Since the mid-1990s, global trafficking in human beings has more than quadrupled and, after an investigation by Europol, is considered to be the crime sector with the highest rate of increase. The business with people is therefore also considered to be the “third most important” – under quotations – criminal source of income after drug and arms trafficking. Just a number of them: Over the last 30 years, 30 million children and women in Southeast Asia alone have been victims of human traffickers – 30 million children and women, this is also an incredible number! Trafficking in human beings, even if I give my previous speakers, is a sum of human rights violations and violates inalienable rights such as the right to life, freedom, equality, dignity, security, non-discrimination, health and all rights that also concern employment protection. Trafficking in human beings is not only within national borders, but also has an external, global component. In fact, people are usually less developed and traded in more developed regions. For example, people from Moldova are traded primarily in Turkey, Russia or the United Arab Emirates, and the industrialized countries, according to investigations, are very often or most often the endpoint for the victims. Many European countries, which one does not even want to believe, are not just countries of origin, transit or destination. Germany, for example, is both transit and destination country for organised trafficking in human beings. These are mainly people from Eastern Europe who are being exploited mostly in forced prostitution, on construction sites, in the hotel or in the hospitality industry. More than half of the victims associated with slavery come from the South East Asian region, which is why Southeast Asia is considered an international hub of trafficking in human beings. In particular Cambodia, Laos, Thailand and Vietnam are affected. It has already been mentioned and you can't mention it often enough: Most of these people are sold to the sex industry. It's daily in Cambodia alone – I repeat: daily! – 50,000 young women and girls, including many small children who are sexually exploited. Why is this happening, apart from the fact that some deserve it properly? – Because, on the one hand, there is the demand, but on the other hand there are many people who simply have nothing to live. Economic distress, perceptiveness, and belief in a better future make many people a slight prey of traffickers. Abductions and the sale of children are also among the common entry scenarios of victims in human trafficking. However, poverty is not the only reason for dramatic developments, it is also factors such as discrimination to the political persecution or corruption that are added. In human trafficking, as well as in organ trafficking, many actors are involved. That's why it's so hard to get them on the syllables. Advertisers are often familiar or family members, to benefit from swindlers, many middle-men – especially men – but also, of course, customers, consumers, holders and well-structured global networks and criminal organizations. That is why this proposal, as I think, is a very, very important one, as well as the Austrian attitude on this issue, the measures and all the steps to be taken here are very important, even if it is a mammoth task. I am very pleased that we are not only debating this today, but that we are committed not only to combating trafficking in human beings in Austria, but not only to turning Europe into a subject, but also to not losing sight of the global component. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e14276	Next speaker: Mrs Kira Grünberg. - Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e14301	Mr President. Estimated Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seers! In addition, awareness-raising and awareness-raising is promoted in those occupational groups that are potentially in contact with victims of trafficking in human beings. ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e16083	Mrs Köstinger is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). [[Phone:Woman Minister]] – Ms. Minister! - Please, the word is with you.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e16112	Thank you very much, Mr President. Of course, I always remember the time as a Member of this House. [[Abg. Lausch: I think that was intention!]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. In addition to the very interesting debate across the vegetable racket, I may nevertheless be able to take a brief look at the details of the two novels that are being decided today. The first novel concerns the feed law: So far, only paper forms are available for the implementation of official controls on feed and for the transmission of sampling data to the Ages, which is no longer timely in the case of weitem. In the future, a fully computer-supported system will be introduced instead of paper-based control. The digitalisation of feed control modernizes the processing and, above all, the documentation of official control and is also an important complement for the implementation of the AMA control. The transition to digital systems will result in the implementation of the German Federal Government's digital strategy in another area. In addition, the so-called EU Control Regulation also covers the area of feed, which, in accordance with the feed law, is supervised by the Federal Office for Food Safety and, on the other hand, by the respective competent head of state. For this reason, the feed law now requires the appropriate accompanying rules to ensure, in particular, the implementation and enforcement of the EU control regulation in the field of monitoring of the rules, on the one hand, for placing on the market, but also for the production and feeding of feed. These amendments do not require any substantial practical changes to the performance and monitoring activities, but are therefore important in order to be carried out in a proven manner by official feed control. The second novel includes the Plant Protection Act: To this end, it must be said that the current provisions are now formally deleted as the principles addressed to the countries. In the context of the EU Control Regulation already mentioned, the accompanying rules corresponding to the Plant Protection Products Act must be laid down. They shall also ensure the implementation and enforcement of the control Regulation in the field of the monitoring of the rules for the placing on the market of plant protection products. Furthermore, contained data protection provisions are adapted to the changed legal situation by the entry into force of the EU General Data Protection Regulation. Ladies and gentlemen, I am looking forward to a very broad agreement and support from the House.

This means recognizing new loads in time and ensuring quality regarding old loads. Of course, we also want to do information work and bring the subject to the fore, to the population – I have a game with: “Healthy start into life” – because that’s why we make this report. [[showing a brochure entitled “Healthy Start into Life / Chemicals in Everyday Life and Their Effect on Pregnancy, Newborns and Babies”]] This is not a self-interest, but the purpose is to enable us all to have a healthier life in a healthy environment with a health-related environmental monitoring.

It is a very small mosaic stone – indeed, because we had several thousand tons of plastic carrier bags in Austria, which then partly landed in nature, on garbage, partly in the combustion. We have also seen in Austria that in recent years 100 kilograms of plastic have been transported per day across the Danube. Where will this land at the end of the day? – Of course, you can say quite simply: in our food chain. This has been demonstrated by this study. Therefore: It is important that we make decisions based on scientific evidence based on environmental policy. In this way, this first progress report is a good start to real progress in protecting our life resources. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e19403	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear ladies and gentlemen in front of the screens! The environment and the immediate environment of life form an important basis for our health. Our successful civilization has brought it to fruition in its development, which secures our prosperity. Where there's a lot of light, there's also a shadow. We people are conscious in everyday life and unconsciously exposed to numerous chemicals and chemical processes. Thus, our health is in direct correlation with the environment and environmental protection. On the issues of the relationship between the environment and health there have already been many studies in the past, individual initiatives, a lot has been researched and there have been high-quality scientific publications. The Human Biomonitoring Report is linked to this. In terms of content, it is about which pollutants can be detected in the human body and at which concentration they are found. How healthy are we Austrians really? For the first time, the current report gives a collected answer in the form of the merger of the many points – a big picture. This report, which was unanimously adopted by the National Council in 2017, is to be published every two years and brings together the various activities, as they were called by the previous speakers. Softeners, HCB, mercury, lead, heavy metals, microplastics, molds and air pollutants include those substances to which the studies aim. For example, the study found that a softener BPA load in Austria is detectable but lower than in other countries. Now it is about pooling and strategic alignment of efforts in this area, which should preferably be done at European level. A step has been taken in the past EU Presidency under Chancellor Sebastian. We want to continue this course intensively. In conclusion: Unfortunately, the high life expectancy mentioned by me at the outset does not reflect a healthy ageing. In this respect, we still need to catch up in Austria, because old people do not get enough, we have to grow old with quality. Human Biomonitoring is a tool to make Austria better, so that we become a lot more ecological and healthier. Thank you.

Why? - We don't know that very well. But what we know is that a few days later, the reactor was put back into operation. In the meantime, this incident has been clarified two months ago and still has not been resolved. However, ladies and gentlemen, such a rapid shutdown must be important to us, and we must treat it here too. I might bring an example, something comparable, so that you can imagine it. A quick shutdown is like taking a full caracho on the motorway and suddenly putting a full brake. There are, of course, signs of abuse. They are brake pads that take advantage of themselves, and the risk of an accident is increased. That is why we cannot be squandered, and we cannot even be squandered if it happens to the Temelín nuclear power plant, which from the very beginning has been disruptive. You really have to say that. This reactor is made according to Soviet design, it is a Soviet production mixed with US technology. From the beginning, experts have warned about this. But that's exactly what happened: There have been a lot of incidents, sometimes several per year. As I said, this risk nuclear power plant is 50 kilometres away from our border. Going there: No to nuclear power and yes to the elimination of the Temelín nuclear power station! - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e21489	Madam President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors. Ladies and gentlemen! These three plenary sessions sound out with very well-founded Court of Auditors reports, I refer to those on the State Opera. 47 years ago, the State Opera was examined by the Court of Auditors, and as a result, a lot of material had now been produced. What points were particularly striking? I'll start with the ticket sale. For years, it was Usus who passed on ticket quotas to offices that then sold them profitably. Now we are there, I believe, that it cannot be in the sense of the inventor that a state institution passes on cards that someone else sells with profit. According to State Director Meyer, who has now moved to Italy, this practice has been discontinued. I'll stay with the cards for a while. The Court of Auditors has strongly criticised and questioned the transfer of cards to members of the Supervisory Board. Both Mrs. State Secretary Mayer and Mr. State Director – apart from serving in Austria – have clearly demonstrated that it is important for members of the Supervisory Board to be provided with cards, because they would naturally have to take a picture of the performances of the house, starting from the premiere to further performances. It is important that they know about the response of the audience. This card transfer is now carried out in a very dosed, well-dose way. I am referring to another point that, I believe, is up-to-date and relevant, namely the live stream. The state opera, together with the Berlin symphonics, has installed a fee-based model, initially unloved, much criticized, not well rated, but then one has seen: In the last few weeks, it's proved lucky. You couldn't go to the opera, so the opera came home. We have learned that more than 3 million viewers have been reached, more than 260 000 new customers have been able to win, 350 broadcasts worldwide – to Russia, to China et cetera. It is particularly worth mentioning that: 500 schools have been completed in Austria, and I think that is an essential factor for the future. ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e22422	Mr Kainz has been reported to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e22443	Mr President. Thank you, Madam President-in-Office of the Council! High house! Dear spectators at home in front of the screens! I would also like to thank for the informative report. He is very interesting to read and often marvels at what is actually to be read from it. I would like to refer to the Court of Auditors' report on ORF today: Location consolidation – 1. Construction phase, talk. From April 2017 to July 2018, the Court of Auditors reviewed the project Location consolidation of Austrian radio broadcasting in relation to the decisions for the consolidation of the ORF sites, the sale of the ORF radio house, the programme and project organisation, as well as the cost and timing development of the object 1, the office building of the ORF. The period under review covered the years 2012 to 2016. As part of the consolidation of the site, the ORF decided in September 2012 to unite its three locations in Vienna – Argentinarstraße, Heiliglberg and Küniglberg – and to consolidate them at the ORF-Zentrum am Küniglberg. However, from the Court's point of view, this decision was taken without a comprehensive analysis of the advantages and disadvantages of a new building. At that time, a new building in Vienna, Erdberg, would have been in question, in which there would have been no problems with the protection of monuments and a transport connection would have been guaranteed. However, the ORF feared that the public would reject a new location, and so they chose the Küniglberg. Unfortunately, the scope of the renovation work of the building at the Küniglberg site was underestimated. The ORF provided planning services which were not ready to tender, resulting in significant additional costs due to the need for additional orders. In the Court's view, the alerts or service lists were inadequate. However, the ORF responded quickly to misdevelopments and immediately took its master's duties active. Also positive is the sustainability of the media location as well as that there was no production and broadcasting failures throughout the construction phase. In June 2017, the Ministry of the Environment awarded the ORF for the renovation of object 1 with the award of climate protection. However, ladies and gentlemen, it is unfortunate that there is still no transport concept to date. However, it is essential that the ORF and the city of Vienna agree on a common transport concept, because after completion of the project, around 860 employees will be also employed at Küniglberg. On the one hand, the district representative calls for the creation of additional parking spaces at the ORF area to prevent a total parking of the area. On the other hand, the arrival of employees with the car contradicts the transport concepts of the city of Vienna. In line with the Court of Auditors' recommendations, both parties should re-establish their efforts to develop a transport and mobility approach. In this sense, I hope that a solution will be found soon. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e22516	Mr President. Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! I am particularly concerned today about the issue of climate protection in the mammoth project of the ORF site combination. The ORF has focused on climate protection and sustainability in this project. That's great and we welcome Greens of course. There was also praise from the Court of Auditors, which said that such a focus was good because the ORF thus takes into account economic, environmental and social interests as well as the needs of future generations in the pursuit of its business activities. Well, we can join him. This has also been done by the Ministry of the Environment, which the ORF – the colleague has already mentioned – has awarded the Küniglberg climate protection prize – for the renovation of the 1 object on Küniglberg in 2017 – one of these ten buildings and houses that have been renovated at Küniglberg. In the course of this, the ORF, of course, intended to complete further new constructions and renovations according to the same standard. – Beautiful and good, but it doesn’t look like it. Rehabilitation of this one house led to a cost overrun, which amounted to 6,4 million euros in concrete terms. However, since one wanted to remain in the total budget of 303 million euros – and one has to say honestly, reading through the Court of Auditors' report, one can see that even at all corners and ends was not saved – the ORF reduced the level of renovation of two other objects on the Küniglberg. But what does that mean? – That means that there is no proper rehabilitation. Rather, you buy higher running costs, and what is even worse, you buy that you may be running back in a few years and rehabilitating again. In my opinion, this is inefficient and also resource inexplicable. The Court of Auditors report also mentions a ten-year period of use for these buildings. Ten years are relatively few in a renovated building. At the time, the ORF argued that the medium-term financing of the ORF was not guaranteed, which is why it was no longer necessary to plan, and I think it is really very short-sighted, because you have to ask yourself the question of what else you will do with the concrete ruins at Küniglberg. I mean, you can't invest 303 million euros in the conversion of the ORF and say that this must last only ten years, because we don't know how the ORF's funding goes on anyway! In the Committee, the Director-General has tried to condescend, and has meant that climate change equipment is the main issue, and that these would last for a long time over ten years, but I still have to say: I have very little understanding of this. First of all, we talk about a life span of ten years, then we say it's going to last longer, and to give the whole thing a green paint, we're restoring an object very great and we can also award a climate protection price for it. We really have to do much more information in the league – this is my appeal – in order to make everyone really clear: The renovation of buildings must go hand in hand with sustainability and nature conservation, and that must not be just lip recognition! In this sense, thank you very much to the Court of Auditors and also to your team for this report. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-09-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00047_d7e22739	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Let me just say a few short sentences on the ORF site consolidation report: The report has dealt with the first phase of the construction of the ORF site consolidation and is therefore a very timely review. A timely review has the advantage of improving the implementation of outstanding subprojects, which are still under way. We have also made very many recommendations to the ORF. Subjects in our examination were the decision-making process for the consolidation of the sites, the sale of the ORF radio station, the project organisation, the question of Dealing with project disruptions, procurement, cost and timing development and the sustainability of the media location. It has already been stressed that we have seen it positively that the issue of sustainability has played a role. In general, I also agree that it is difficult to deal with construction projects, which are so focused on public interest, without interference, and I just want to address a few criticism points. One of the major criticisms was the inadequate preparation of decisions. For the consolidation of the site in particular was the adverse transport connection to the Küniglberg and the insufficient dedication. We are also concerned with timely planning. So we could have looked at Plan B earlier, namely, to plan within the existing dedication. We recommend that there be discussions again about an overarching transport and mobility concept for the Küniglberg, because with Plan B the number of employees on Küniglberg will also increase significantly. We have also raised the question of whether there is a right of referral by the municipal representation if there are applications for a change of dedication so that decisions can also be made. One of the areas of problem was the high time pressure one has faced, which one has partly indebted itself. The sale of the radio house could not be done in time because of the lack of dedication. As far as the project was concerned, we have highlighted positively that problems that have always occurred have always been responded to. There is a comprehensive guide from the Court of Auditors for carrying out the task of building contractors on the part of the public authorities. We recommend this for all public builders. This is about write-out planning, write-out-only service directories, which are very important. On cost development: The cost target for object 1 was set in advance on the basis of pre-design plans. Accordingly, it is not likely to be met by 6.4 million euros. It has already been mentioned that there is an overall cost framework for the overall project and that one is looking for savings. One area is the lower rehabilitation depth for other objects, and of course we assess this in terms of possible higher maintenance expenses. This should also be considered as a certain risk. Finally, I would like to say that the ORF is for us a contracting authority within the meaning of the Federal Procurement Act until the European Court of Justice takes a different decision. - Thank you.
